| sau_spray | saurischia | saurischia | The spray ground at the foot of the Veil: always wet, deep moss, ferns and dripping tree ferns. |
| sau_swamp | saurischia | saurischia | A dawn redwood swamp in the wet bottom west of the Lessa: buttressed red trunks over ferns and wet moss. |
| sau_thunderfield | saurischia | saurischia | Thunderfield: open grass to the north horizon, a lone monkey-puzzle here and there, where the horned and crested herds graze and the long-necks nest. |
| sau_titan | saurischia | saurischia | The Titan Stand: the oldest monkey-puzzles in the country, enormous grey columns standing far apart over a floor of ferns and deep moss. |
| sawgrass | lowmouth | gannet, jadewater, kaldvik, lowmouth, lowmouth_bayou, ribshore, thornpan, thornpan_delta | The sawgrass flats: a sheet of grass standing in a hand or two of clear water, running to the sky, with hammocks of palm and oak like islands. |
| scarp_face | windbarrow | windbarrow | The face of the Barrow Edge and the walls of its combes: bare white chalk with the flint band running level along it, junipers out of the cracks, lichen. |
| scree_summit | glassfall | glassfall |  |
| seabed | glassfall | glassfall, hemlow, hemlow_lanes, kaldvik, kaldvik_fjord, megalith, peregrine, saurischia, windbarrow, zoo | Under the sea. |
| slickrock | redrock | redrock, vermilion | Bare sloping sandstone: fins, domes, canyon benches. A juniper in a crack here and there. |
| space_void | starfall | starfall | The ground under a map that floats in space (`sky space`): it lies below the world's floor and is never built, so nothing grows or lives on it. Every plant or animal a biome put there would hang in the void under the map. |
| steading | windbarrow | windbarrow | The ground of a Windbarrow village: yards trodden to bare chalk between the round houses, barley plots behind hurdles, hearths, racks and pens, cattle and sheep standing about. |
| steppe_deck | megalith | megalith | The chalk steppe an arena is sunk into, after Windbarrow's downs: short turf with the chalk coming through, thyme, rock-rose and lichen and nothing taller, starlings flocking over it, crows and an eagle in the sky. It dresses the plain round a buried arena, not the arena itself; what is inside comes from the materials the steppe style paints on the structure. |
| swale | windbarrow | windbarrow | The damp bottoms of the dry valleys, where the soil is deep: feathergrass shoulder-high and greener, big tussocks, a birch or a willow in the wettest heads. |
| talus_slope | vermilion | vermilion | The steep debris slopes under red cliffs, between the bare rock of the faces and the flat desert below: red sand and broken sandstone, juniper and a little sagebrush. |
| tepe_ground | windbarrow | windbarrow | The hilltop round the Tepe: turf grazed and trodden down to the chalk, thyme and thistles at the wall's foot, and the pillars standing in it. The bare rubble is the enclosure's own floor, inside the bench wall. |
| town_barley | - | no shipped world | A generated town's field (region barley): barley_plot, with its edge exactly at the wall instead of ragged. |
| town_hay | - | kitehold | A generated town's field (region hay): gh_hayfield, with its edge exactly at the wall instead of ragged. |
| town_pasture | - | kitehold | A generated town's field (region pasture): gh_pasture, with its edge exactly at the wall instead of ragged. |
| town_yard | - | no shipped world | Back gardens and the green between a generated town's houses: lawn, a few fruit trees and shrubs, kept low so the roofs read from above. |
| turquoise_water | saltmirror | saltmirror | The Turquoise: brine fed from below in the north-west of the pan, a metre or two deep over pale mud and so clear and so blue-green that it looks lit from underneath, with smaller eyes round it going down further than you can see. |
| va_town | bonebight | bonebight | Vila Azul's streets and gardens on the hill: short turf between tiled walls, blue hydrangea hedges, a palm or a fig here and there. |
| village_green | glassfall | glassfall | Old fields and yards around a settlement, gone to meadow. |
| white_dunes | saltmirror | saltmirror, saltmirror_array | The white dunes: gypsum piled against the range by the south-west wind, soaptree yuccas on the crests, rabbitbrush in the hollows and pale lizards that match the sand. |
| wisp_moss | - | glassfall, hemlow, lowmouth | The Wisp Moss round Hemmere: sphagnum and peat pools, reedmace, willow scrub, dead alders standing in the wet; marsh lights over it after sunset. |
| works_yard | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, slagdeck, sluice | The gravel apron round the arena deck: ash, clinker and nothing growing. Nothing to look at, which is the point; everything worth seeing is inside. |
| zoo_lawn | zoo | zoo | The zoo's lawn: short lawn grass, so lying animals show, a few lone hawthorns for perching and climbing birds, and lupine for the bees and hummingbirds. No creatures of its own; the zoo puts them out (src/creatures/zoo.rs). |

| cloud_forest | base | kitehold | Mountain forest wrapped in cloud: mossy, wet and cool. |

## Weapons

| name | from | used by | notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| anti_materiel_rifle | realistic | unused | A fifty-calibre rifle made for engine blocks rather than men. Five in the magazine, slow, and it goes through a metre of wall and whoever is behind it, hollowing the way. The right button is a four-power sight. There is no such thing as cover from it, which is the point of carrying something this heavy. |
| arc_gun | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, sluice, starfall, transmission | Two beams off one tank, and the left button is the one people remember: a rope of electric blue with a thread of it turning fast round the outside, held on a man rather than fired at him. It does its damage by the second, so the fight is not about timing a shot but about keeping a line on somebody who is strafing, and it falls off hard past thirty metres. The right button is the same stream let loose: shorter, and once it is on somebody it does not stay there. It jumps to whoever is nearest them within seven metres and in sight, and on from them to the next, four times, losing half of itself at each jump. Held on one of a crowd, it is in all of them, and it is the reason nobody in this arena stands close to a friend. |
| assault_rifle | realistic | unused | The rifle every army carries: thirty rounds, fully automatic, good from across a room to across a street. The left button holds it on automatic; the right fires three-round bursts that stay tighter. It takes the same rounds as the machine gun, out of one store. |
| bolt_rifle | realistic | unused | A bolt-action rifle with a ten-power scope: five rounds, one at a time, and one in the head is a kill at any range. Nothing in the realistic kit reaches further. The right button is the scope. |
| bore | slagdeck | duelbox, hemlow, megalith, porch, slagdeck, starfall, transmission | A rock drill with the feed taken off it, so the whole of what it does goes down the bore. It does not fire rounds: hold the left button and it puts out a turning rope of cutting light that takes a ball out of whatever it is held on, over and over, until the hole is through and the hole is a sightline. The right button runs the drill backwards: it throws what the left button cut out of the walls back out of the gun as half-metre blocks of the map's own rock, lobbed across the room, and where one lands it stays. Wall up a doorway, build a step to a ledge, or drop one on somebody's head, which is the only one of the three that does not leave a block behind. It is the only weapon in the arena that changes the arena, and the reason nobody holds a corner for long. |
| digger | slagdeck | duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, starfall, transmission | A pneumatic blast hammer off a shelf in the works: a ram that slams whatever its head is pressed against, with a spade welded under the head. No bore, no rounds, nothing to run out of: you squeeze the handle and about three metres in front of you ceases to be a problem. Because it takes none of its own blast you can put the head against the floor and ride the shove onto the gantry, which is the only way up there that costs you no health. The spade bites whatever the head is against while the handle is squeezed, at the rate a shovel works out in the world, so a body down a borer hole can cut its own way out rather than be killed in it. The right button is a shorter, weaker squeeze that throws whoever it catches much further: a shove for getting somebody off a ledge rather than for killing them. |
| flamethrower | realistic | unused | Burning fuel in a stream, held on whatever is in front of you for as long as the trigger is: damage by the second, nine metres of it, and nothing in the tank lasts long. The right button lobs a gout of fuel that bursts into fire where it lands, for a doorway or a crowd. |
| goo_gun | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, sluice, starfall, transmission | A tank of sludge thrown in lobbing globs. A glob that finds a body bursts on it; a glob that finds anything else lands as real voxels of sludge -- it clings where it touches, slumps when nothing holds it up, stacks on the globs already there, and sets them off when it goes. That is what the gun is for: not winning a duel but making a doorway, a ramp or a pad cost whoever uses it next. Holding the right button grows one glob instead of throwing six, up to three times the burst, and keeps it loaded for as long as the button is held. It goes when the button comes up, and leaves the muzzle harder, so a full one carries -- a fat, floating glob, slower than the little ones and lobbed rather than thrown. |
| grenade_launcher | realistic | unused | Forty-millimetre grenades lobbed in an arc. The left button fires one that goes off on whatever it hits first; the right fires one that bounces about and goes off when its fuse runs out, for the room you cannot see into. Slow to reload and very much your own problem at close range. |
| knife | realistic | unused | A fixed blade. Shorter reach than the shovel and less in each blow, but it is out and back again three times while the shovel goes once. The right button is a stab held back for a moment that goes through to the heart: twice the damage, and half again to the head like the cut. |
| machine_gun | realistic | unused | A light machine gun: a hundred rounds before it wants feeding and a slow reload when it does. Less accurate than the rifle and it climbs as it goes, but nothing else puts as many rounds down a corridor. It takes the assault rifle's rounds. |
| minigun | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, sluice, starfall, transmission | Six barrels on a gearbox: it takes half a second to come up to speed, then holds a wall of bullets on a target. Weak per round and it wanders, so it is a weapon for holding a corridor, not for duels across the hall. Belt-fed from the drum, so it never reloads: the spin-up is the wait it has instead. The right button fires all six barrels at once: a ring of burning flechettes, six rounds off the drum, at the scrap gun's pace. Each one hits harder than a bullet and bites a voxel out of whatever it strikes; the ring holds together inside ten metres and opens round a target past it. |
| pistol | realistic | unused | A service pistol: fifteen in the magazine, a quick reload, and as fast as you can pull the trigger. Accurate enough to be worth aiming and worth more to the head than anywhere else. The right button is a steadier aimed shot at a slower pace. It is what you come back with. |
| rocket_launcher | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, sluice, starfall, transmission | A straight tube: the rocket flies level and bursts on whatever it meets. It takes a third of its own blast, which is enough to rocket-jump onto the catwalk and not quite enough to kill you doing it. One in the tube and a second to reload, so a rocket already away is no reason to wait -- three of them can be in the air at once. The right button fires a guided rocket, a little slower and a fifth weaker, that turns after wherever you are pointing -- not at once, it swings round behind your aim -- so you can fly it round a corner or walk it onto a man who dodged. |
| rpg | realistic | unused | A shoulder-fired rocket: one in the tube, a long reload, and a burst that takes a wall's worth of voxels out of what it hits. It flies straight and a little slower than the arena's, and you take your own blast in full, so it is not something to fire at a man in arm's reach. |
| scrap_gun | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, sluice, starfall, transmission | A hopper of scrap fired as a cone of nine shards. They are bodies, not rays: you can watch them go, they ricochet once off anything they clip, and they are gone less than a second out, which is what makes the gun murderous inside ten metres and useless past thirty. The right button lobs the shell whole: it bounces once, bursts, and the burst throws the hopper's own scrap out of it -- ten of the same shards, going nowhere in particular and gone in less than a second, so what the shot leaves behind it is the cone it would have fired, pointing every way at once. |
| shotgun | realistic | unused | Nine pellets of buckshot at a time: across a room it kills, across a hall it stings. The left button is the pump, one shot and rack it; the right fires a slug, one heavy ball that holds together to range. Eight in the tube, loaded a shell at a time. |
| shovel | realistic | unused | A long-handled shovel. No rounds and nothing to run out of: the left button swings the blade at whatever is within reach, and while it is held the blade bites the ground or the wall in front of you the way the Digger's spade does, so you can dig yourself a trench, cut a step up a bank or take a wall down a shovelful at a time. The right button is a slower two-handed swing you wind up by holding it, worth nearly twice as much. Half again to the head either way. |
| sidearm | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, sluice, starfall, transmission | The gun you are left with: a stubby bullpup with its magazine along the top, accurate enough to be worth aiming and light enough that you go looking for something else. It rewards a head shot by half again. The right button fires bursts of six in rapid succession with a beat between them; the muzzle climbs through a burst, so the first round goes where you aimed and the last ones wander, which makes it a close-range answer. It takes the same bullets as the minigun, out of one store: rounds fired from either are gone from both, and a pad for either fills both. |
| skipjack | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, sluice, starfall, transmission | A bright plastic thing among nine iron ones, and the only weapon in the arena anybody laughs at before it kills them. Razor discs off a drum, flat and level, ricocheting four times and keeping most of its speed through each, so it is for shooting round a corner at somebody who thinks a corner is cover -- aim at the wall beside them and the disc does the rest. The right button lobs the drum itself, which arcs, bursts where it lands and throws ten little discs out of the burst -- they bounce, they scatter, and for the next two seconds the floor of that room is not somewhere anybody wants to be standing. It is called the skipjack for what the discs do off a wall, and nobody now remembers what it was for before somebody put blades in it. |
| sniper_rifle | slagdeck | ctfbox, duelbox, megalith, porch, slagdeck, sluice, starfall, transmission | One shot, four hundred metres, no falloff, and twice the damage in the top of a body: a head shot kills outright and anything else leaves a man who has to go and find a health pack. The right button is the sight and a charge at once: hold it and the view narrows to a third while the shot winds up over a second and a bit; the left button fires it, and letting the sight down without firing throws the charge away. A full charge hits half as hard again and goes through up to a metre and a quarter of wall, hollowing a channel as it goes, so a man behind thin cover is not behind cover; a tap is an ordinary shot. Five in the magazine and two and a bit seconds to fill it, which is the whole of its weakness: miss twice in a corridor and whoever you missed is on top of you. |
